The report was checked against [Botermann et al., arXiv:1409.7951v1](https://arxiv.org/pdf/1409.7951v1), printed pp. 1–4: equations (1)–(4), apparatus/processing text, Figure 2 and Table I. Its calibration and fitting account supports the recorded apparatus summary and reported uncertainty scales. No underlying likelihood, fit-weight construction, covariance or coverage was supplied by the paper; the item preserves those limits. This review does not independently validate borrowed transition-frequency measurements. The comparison requires additional resonance and active-transition assumptions and an explicitly stipulated inertial reception idealization. It tests a stated alternative, not all classical physics.

Independent exact arithmetic verifies the positive-frequency inverse, interval endpoints 1719/18281 and 2079/17921, difference 7200000/327613801, and the nominal alternative-prediction bounds 0.062<Q1<0.063. Decimal evaluation of rounded frequency centers gives Q≈1.4744849565332854e-9. The Galilean example table at C=10,30,100 agrees to its displayed precision. No fabricated sampling model, significance or empirical certainty is introduced.

A historical frontier-ledger normal-collar finding was encountered. ## Further current SR citation checks

Their cross-batch frame/causal/sign conventions match the exact earlier definitions. The tensor laws require total differentiability where asserted and distinguish numerical component arrays from intrinsic contracted tensors; the affine-isometry argument uses global R4 and C2, not a local chart or partial derivatives alone.
